# Ilse Kordan's Takes on Recent Bills

## NGESAA 2064
**Proposer:** eruween

### What Ilse Liked:
> I support the strict enforcement of fiscal transparency and the mandate for budgetary compliance, as sound public finance is a prerequisite for a stable, market-based social order that protects the taxpayer from regional mismanagement.

### What Ilse Disliked:
> I am deeply concerned by the centralizing of operational authority and the erosion of regional autonomy; as an Ordoliberal, I believe in subsidiarity and fear that stripping regions of their administrative agency creates a top-heavy, bureaucratic state that lacks the flexibility to respond to local economic needs.

---

## Establishment of the Environmental Protection Agency Act 2064. By Sebil and AKY
**Proposer:** matiyuaaa

### What Ilse Liked:
> The introduction of a clear, stable regulatory framework provides the 'rules of the game' necessary for a functioning social market economy, preventing externalities from distorting competition.

### What Ilse Disliked:
> The proposal lacks specific details on how to prevent bureaucratic overreach or ensure that compliance costs do not disproportionately burden our small-to-medium enterprises and the working class.

---

## proposed 2063 budget to have the following changes: Defense 2.43% | Healthcare 4.23% | Climate & Environment 0.63% | Education 4.76%
**Proposer:** magicalmeowgician

### What Ilse Liked:
> I applaud the prioritization of education and healthcare spending, as investing in human capital is the foundation of a stable, competitive social market economy.

### What Ilse Disliked:
> The meager 0.63% allocation for climate and environment is insufficient, failing to uphold the Ordoliberal principle of state responsibility for long-term ecological sustainability and market stability.

---

## 2063 budget
**Proposer:** uzkalnis

### What Ilse Liked:
> Nothing

### What Ilse Disliked:
> The bill text is entirely missing, making it impossible to assess whether this budget maintains fiscal discipline, avoids inflationary deficits, or adequately provides for the social safety net as required by my Ordoliberal and Social Democratic principles.

---

## Adopt the National Childcare Tax Credit Act (https://docs.google.com/document/d/1rDqRBNMt4bV8Bm7IuNqi37aQIGXRD72Ip9lhD3kboo0/edit?usp=sharing)
**Proposer:** magicalmeowgician

### What Ilse Liked:
> I appreciate the targeted approach to supporting working-class families and the inclusion of a refund mechanism for those with low tax liability, which adheres to the Ordoliberal principle of ensuring social stability without market distortion.

### What Ilse Disliked:
> The rigid income threshold and the potential for the administrative registry to become overly bureaucratic risk creating 'welfare traps' that may inadvertently discourage workforce participation near the phase-out limit.

---

## Amend Section 2 of the Fiscal Resilience and Public Investment Act to read: In any fiscal year in which nominal GDP growth is positive, the budget deficit shall not exceed nominal GDP growth multiplied by the following factor, determined by public debt-to-GDP at the close of the immediately preceding fiscal year: Below sixty percent (60.00%): one and one-half times (1.5x) nominal GDP growth; Sixty percent (60.00%) to eighty percent (80.00%): one and two-tenths times (1.2x) nominal GDP growth; Above eighty percent (80.00%): one times (1.0x) nominal GDP growth. In any fiscal year in which nominal GDP growth is negative, the budget deficit shall not exceed three percent (3.00%) of Caprican nominal GDP for that fiscal year, irrespective of the debt-to-GDP tier otherwise applicable under Section 2(1). The budget surplus in any fiscal year shall not exceed one percent (1.00%) of Caprican nominal GDP for that fiscal year.
**Proposer:** magicalmeowgician

### What Ilse Liked:
> I appreciate the implementation of a clear, predictable 'ordos' framework that prevents arbitrary fiscal expansion and enforces fiscal discipline, as stable public finances are a prerequisite for a social market economy.

### What Ilse Disliked:
> The strict capping of surpluses at one percent may limit the state's ability to build necessary rainy-day reserves during periods of growth, potentially undermining long-term social investment capacities.

---

## Adopt the Fiscal Resilience and Public Investment Act, 2064 (https://docs.google.com/document/d/1uYE1yZ_A3q0CI78uqHm8KruFpn5YoBzbxExfmYrDrCM/edit?usp=sharing)
**Proposer:** magicalmeowgician

### What Ilse Liked:
> I strongly support the commitment to long-term fiscal sustainability and the 'debt brake' mechanism, which aligns with the Ordoliberal principle that a stable monetary and fiscal framework is a prerequisite for a functioning social market economy. Furthermore, the constitutional-style protection of labor rights and essential social services provides the necessary floor for social cohesion without sacrificing macroeconomic discipline.

### What Ilse Disliked:
> I am concerned that the rigid, formulaic nature of the spending floors—particularly the lack of flexibility in the 'Priority Squeeze Mitigation Protocol'—could create dangerous budgetary externalities that force excessive cuts elsewhere during cyclical downturns, potentially undermining the state's capacity to deliver other vital public goods.

---

## Adopt the National Ecosystem Banking Program and Wetland and Coastal Protection and Conservation Act, 2063 (https://docs.google.com/document/d/1Lao5JDXs0DPDWfyyGLlKsN-A7RlR0wP4JpGYMeqHjr4/edit?usp=sharing)
**Proposer:** magicalmeowgician

### What Ilse Liked:
> I appreciate the Ordoliberal application of a structured, transparent regulatory framework that utilizes market mechanisms—like standardized credits and transaction fees—to internalize environmental costs, while simultaneously ensuring robust state oversight through the Ministry to prevent market failure.

### What Ilse Disliked:
> I am concerned that the reliance on private 'Banking Instruments' and market-based credit trading could lead to a 'pay-to-pollute' reality, potentially prioritizing corporate efficiency over the genuine, non-fungible ecological well-being of the working-class communities who rely on these local natural resources.

---

## Life Preservation Act, 2063
**Proposer:** aalborg

### What Ilse Liked:
> I appreciate the bill’s attempt to provide legal clarity and safeguard medical practitioners from liability when acting in good faith, which aligns with an Ordoliberal emphasis on clear, predictable rules for a functioning social and economic order.

### What Ilse Disliked:
> I am deeply concerned by the punitive nature of sections 8 and 9, which introduce criminal charges for medical decisions; as a Social Democrat, I believe health policy should prioritize patient autonomy and medical ethics over the criminalization of healthcare practitioners.

---

## Kaoqing Communique Implementation Act, 2063
**Proposer:** aalborg

### What Ilse Liked:
> I support the movement toward international cooperation and the removal of artificial trade barriers, as the resumption of normal commercial relations is vital for the prosperity of the Caprican working class and the stability of our market order.

### What Ilse Disliked:
> I am concerned by the potential for executive overreach in the unchecked power to lift financial restrictions and the ambiguous legal status of the Representative Office in Yuanlin, which threatens the transparency and democratic accountability required by an Ordoliberal framework.

---

## National Animal Protection Act, 2063
**Proposer:** aalborg

### What Ilse Liked:
> I appreciate the bill's focus on clear, enforceable rules that prevent the exploitation of common natural resources, which aligns with the Ordoliberal principle of protecting the integrity of our shared ecosystem through state-guaranteed order.

### What Ilse Disliked:
> I am concerned that the bill fails to account for the potential economic displacement of traditional hunters or small-scale trappers who may rely on these regions for their livelihood; it lacks provisions for transition support or clear guidance on how these regulations interface with private property and existing labor practices.

---

## Adopt the Legal Status of the Shadow Cabinet of the Opposition Act (https://docs.google.com/document/d/10Bwlisf2piZZ3wCCW9zBPA1GENjtfW5qaC2giu1kLFY/edit?usp=sharing)
**Proposer:** magicalmeowgician

### What Ilse Liked:
> As an Ordoliberal, I appreciate the institutionalization of clear, transparent checks and balances, as a strong opposition provides the necessary market-like competition of ideas that keeps the government accountable and prevents the monopolization of power.

### What Ilse Disliked:
> The bill creates a rigid, mandatory bureaucratic structure for opposition parties that risks over-formalizing political dissent, potentially entrenching an adversarial partisanship that conflicts with the social democratic need for consensus-based, pragmatic governance for the working class.

---

## Make all elections (on saturdays irl) on 5 December xxxx (because that is the time the ballot opens instead of 9 November) and have all MPs, Presidents, Governors, etc. take office on 15 March of the following year (because that is roughly when the results come out on that monday after the election).
**Proposer:** oak_cal

### What Ilse Liked:
> I appreciate the administrative clarity and the establishment of a predictable, orderly transition process, which supports the Ordoliberal principle of a stable, rules-based framework for governance.

### What Ilse Disliked:
> I am concerned that the extended transition period between December and March creates a significant 'lame duck' window that could impede timely legislative responses to economic crises, undermining the efficiency of the state.

---

## *GDNR to edit Section 3, Clause 1:* A region may impose an income tax on individuals within its lawful constitutional authority. Where an individual earns income from employment performed across multiple regions, taxation shall be apportioned according to the location in which the taxable work is performed, subject to nationally established rules preventing double taxation.
**Proposer:** oak_cal

### What Ilse Liked:
> I support the establishment of clear, nationally consistent rules that prevent double taxation, as this provides the legal certainty and market order necessary for a functioning social market economy.

### What Ilse Disliked:
> Nothing.

---

## *Amendment the GDNR to add in Section 3:* Nothing in this Act shall prohibit a region from imposing, modifying, or repealing taxes within its lawful constitutional authority, provided that such taxes are applied without discrimination based on the taxpayer's region of incorporation, headquarters, residence, or origin, and do not impose an undue burden on interstate commerce.
**Proposer:** oak_cal

### What Ilse Liked:
> The emphasis on non-discrimination and the protection of the internal market aligns with Ordoliberal principles by ensuring a level playing field and preventing protectionist regional fragmentation.

### What Ilse Disliked:
> The language regarding 'undue burden' is dangerously vague and could be exploited by corporate interests to challenge necessary social democratic fiscal policies or regional labor protections in the courts.

---

## Gradual Demonopolization and New Rules Act (GDNR)
**Proposer:** eruween

### What Ilse Liked:
> I support the emphasis on transparent, competitively neutral regulations and the removal of regional protectionist barriers, as these align with the Ordoliberal priority of maintaining a functional competitive order that prevents the abuse of market power.

### What Ilse Disliked:
> I am wary of the rapid one-year transition period which risks service disruption for the working class, and I am concerned that the legislation lacks sufficient safeguards to ensure that private providers prioritize the universal service obligations historically upheld by state monopolies.

---

## Transport Market Devolution (Capital Area Transit Authority) Amendment Act, 2062
**Proposer:** uzkalnis

### What Ilse Liked:
> I support the emphasis on competitive, transparent, and non-discriminatory concession procedures, which aligns with the Ordoliberal requirement for a fair market order, as well as the protection of labor rights for transferring employees, which is essential for Social Democratic stability.

### What Ilse Disliked:
> I am concerned that regional fiscal responsibility for transport deficits could undermine territorial cohesion if federal equalization grants prove insufficient, potentially leading to a 'postcode lottery' of service quality that leaves the working class in poorer regions at a disadvantage.

---

## Explanatory Note Act (https://docs.google.com/document/d/1375ZMlVGk6xn2Fi6ABzZ4HDLGBVberx_nwuFZcVQ6kU/edit?usp=sharing)
**Proposer:** adieliicious

### What Ilse Liked:
> As an Ordoliberal, I strongly support the increase in transparency and the formalization of rules surrounding the legislative process, as it prevents arbitrary governance and ensures that the public can understand the legal framework governing the market and social order.

### What Ilse Disliked:
> The strict 2500-symbol limit on explanatory notes may inadvertently lead to oversimplification or a lack of necessary depth for complex economic reforms, potentially hindering a truly thorough analysis of how a bill affects the working class.

---

## Adopt the Explanatory Note Act (https://docs.google.com/document/d/1375ZMlVGk6xn2Fi6ABzZ4HDLGBVberx_nwuFZcVQ6kU/edit?usp=sharing)
**Proposer:** _sedan_

### What Ilse Liked:
> As an Ordoliberal, I strongly support this increase in procedural transparency; requiring clear, accessible explanations of law fosters a rules-based order where citizens can hold their representatives accountable and understand the state's interventions in the market.

### What Ilse Disliked:
> The strict 2500-character limit on the explanatory note may prove overly rigid for complex economic legislation, potentially forcing politicians to oversimplify crucial details regarding the social market economy.

---

## The Government of Caprica hereby designates Himat Sahil Khaskaar (HSK) as a terrorist organization. All funds, assets, and economic resources belonging to or controlled by HSK, its leadership, or affiliated entities within Caprican jurisdiction shall be frozen. Caprican citizens, companies, financial institutions, and other organizations are prohibited from conducting business with, supplying goods or services to, financing, or otherwise providing material support to HSK or its affiliates.
**Proposer:** aalborg

### What Ilse Liked:
> As an Ordoliberal, I support the state's responsibility to uphold the rule of law and maintain a secure economic order by restricting entities that fundamentally threaten the stability and safety of our market framework.

### What Ilse Disliked:
> The bill lacks explicit provisions for independent judicial oversight in the asset-freezing process, which risks infringing upon the principle of legal certainty and due process that must underpin any state intervention.

---

### What Ilse Disliked:
> I am concerned by the lack of provided documentation regarding his specific policy mandate, as the Ordoliberal tradition demands transparency and strict institutional oversight to prevent the concentration of unchecked executive power.

**Proposer:** eruween

### What Ilse Liked:
> I support the emphasis on parliamentary oversight and the rule of law, which serves to prevent the executive branch from exercising arbitrary power in foreign and military affairs.

> The bill potentially introduces excessive bureaucratic hurdles that could impede the state's capacity to act decisively in urgent national security scenarios, threatening the stability required for a functioning market economy.

## Aiding Kuliga with 8 NSM anti ship missile launchers and 60 missiles
**Proposer:** eruween

### What Ilse Liked:
> I support the principle of subsidiarity and self-defense for sovereign entities, as providing defensive tools allows Kuliga to maintain the stability necessary for a functioning social market economy.

### What Ilse Disliked:
> I am concerned by the lack of transparent budgetary oversight regarding the procurement costs and the potential for these arms to provoke an inflationary military escalation that diverts essential resources away from domestic social welfare programs.

---

## National Transportation and Infrastructure Modernization Act
**Proposer:** adieliicious

### What Ilse Liked:
> As an Ordoliberal, I strongly support the prioritization of infrastructure maintenance through the Infrastructure Preservation Fund, as it promotes long-term economic efficiency and safety. Furthermore, as a Social Democrat, I applaud the commitment to public, non-profit passenger rail, which ensures essential social mobility and provides reliable transit for the working class.

### What Ilse Disliked:
> The bill lacks a clear framework for competitive bidding and private sector participation in the non-rail infrastructure projects, risking potential government inefficiency and fiscal opacity. I am also concerned that the massive scale of these projects may lead to 'pork-barrel' spending without sufficient cost-benefit oversight.

---

## National Transportation and Infrastructure Modernization Act
**Proposer:** adieliicious

### What Ilse Liked:
> As an Ordoliberal, I strongly support the commitment to mandatory infrastructure maintenance and the creation of a dedicated Infrastructure Preservation Fund, which ensures the long-term utility of public assets; furthermore, as a Social Democrat, I applaud the state ownership of the passenger rail network as a public service that fosters equitable social mobility.

### What Ilse Disliked:
> The bill lacks a clear framework for competitive bidding and private sector integration in the construction phase, which risks inefficiency; I am also concerned that such a massive surge in government spending, without clear fiscal sustainability measures or a mechanism to prevent market distortion, could place an undue burden on the national budget.

---

## National Transportation and Infrastructure Modernization Act
**Proposer:** oak_cal

### What Ilse Liked:
> I strongly support the focus on maintaining existing infrastructure through the 40% budgetary mandate, as well as the creation of a public, non-profit rail system that ensures essential mobility services are not subjected to the whims of private rent-seeking.

### What Ilse Disliked:
> The bill lacks a clear framework for competitive bidding and independent oversight in the construction phases, which risks inefficient capital allocation and cronyism that undermines the ordoliberal principle of a fair, rule-based competitive market.

---

## NSI
**Proposer:** alanmu0106

### What Ilse Liked:
> I support the establishment of mandatory civilian oversight and the transparency requirements imposed on national security bodies, as these check the potential for unchecked executive power and align with the Ordoliberal requirement for clear, rule-based governance.

### What Ilse Disliked:
> I am concerned that the power given to the Prime Minister to select which parliamentary chamber confirms the Directors of COIN and CIVIC undermines robust legislative scrutiny; a truly accountable system should not allow the executive to 'shop' for the most compliant confirmation body.

---

## Fishermen Safety and Seafood Health Act
**Proposer:** oak_cal

### What Ilse Liked:
> I strongly support the focus on empowering independent fishing businesses through low-interest loans and safety grants, which reflects the Ordoliberal principle of maintaining a competitive market of small-scale entrepreneurs rather than allowing industry consolidation, alongside the essential social protections for workers.

### What Ilse Disliked:
> I am concerned that the breadth of the ministerial oversight could lead to excessive bureaucratic interference; the government must ensure these regulations provide a stable framework for competition rather than stifling the autonomy of fishing operators with unnecessary red tape.

---

### What Ilse Disliked:
> I am concerned that the legislative reliance on centralized Ministry authority to command interbasin transfers and dictate funding tranches risks undermining local autonomy and creating bureaucratic distortions that hinder efficient resource allocation.

## Amend Article III Section II of the constitution with following changes: ﻿Add §II(g) with the following text: "No government structure can have powers over both internal security and external intelligence in one time. Any structure that conducts operations in one of these spheres can't conduct any operations in another one. Only in case of concrete existential threat (war, military actions of any kind or foreign intervention of any kind) both government structures can cooperate and conduct operations together."
**Proposer:** _sedan_

### What Ilse Liked:
> I strongly support the principle of institutional checks and balances; preventing the concentration of surveillance power is essential to protecting the rights of the working class and maintaining a competitive, transparent state order.

### What Ilse Disliked:
> The vague definition of a 'concrete existential threat' could be exploited by executive powers to bypass these safeguards, potentially allowing for the very centralization of authority the bill aims to prevent.

---

## Amendment to CMRA
**Proposer:** alanmu0106

### What Ilse Liked:
> I approve of the clear stipulations for basic labor protections, such as minimum wage and safe working conditions, as these safeguard the dignity of the working class against exploitation, while the inclusion of a pathway to regularized status aligns with an orderly, rules-based approach to integration.

### What Ilse Disliked:
> I am concerned that decentralizing recruitment and placement to regional authorities risks creating a fragmented labor market that could undermine national wage standards and lead to a 'race to the bottom' if regional monitoring proves inconsistent or lax.

---

## Budget 2058
**Proposer:** uzkalnis

## National Aviation Act
**Proposer:** alanmu0106

### What Ilse Liked:
> I strongly support the rigorous Ordoliberal framework established in Sections 3 and 4, which mandates transparent, competition-based access to airport facilities and prevents protectionist regional barriers. Furthermore, the Social Democratic commitment to a Remote and Underserved Air Service Fund ensures that regional equity and public connectivity are maintained through transparent, competitive tenders rather than opaque subsidies.

### What Ilse Disliked:
> I am concerned by the potential for fiscal inefficiency; while investment in infrastructure is vital, the government must ensure that the ₳24 billion appropriation is subject to strict performance audits to prevent 'gold-plating' of terminals at the expense of taxpayers and to ensure that state-funded infrastructure truly serves the public interest rather than the interests of specific, well-connected airline operators.

---

## National Police Standard Act
**Proposer:** alanmu0106

### What Ilse Liked:
> I strongly support the rigorous focus on merit-based recruitment and the prohibition of cronyism; it aligns perfectly with the Ordoliberal requirement for a state that functions under clear, impartial rules rather than private interests. Additionally, the mandate for professional training and civil liberty protections is a necessary step to ensure the state remains a protector of the citizen, fostering the social trust essential for a stable market economy.

### What Ilse Disliked:
> While the goals are commendable, I am concerned that the rigid imposition of staffing ratios and the creation of a massive, centralized National Police Standards and Integrity Commission could lead to bureaucratic overreach. It risks infringing upon the local operational flexibility that is vital for effective community-level governance, potentially replacing one form of inefficient patronage with a distant, unchecked national technocracy.

---

## amend the senate abolition amendment article 2 section I subsection 4) : change "A further 13 representatives will be elected by national closed-list voting." to "A further 16 representatives will be elected by national closed-list voting, determined via the hare-quota.
**Proposer:** deleted_user_a09cee4ed28d

### What Ilse Liked:
> The adoption of the Hare quota is a positive step toward electoral fairness, as it is generally more proportional and better ensures that legislative representation accurately mirrors the popular vote, which aligns with my commitment to democratic equity.

### What Ilse Disliked:
> I am concerned that expanding the size of the legislature through closed-list appointments strengthens party apparatuses at the expense of individual candidate accountability to the electorate, potentially weakening the direct link between the working class and their representatives.

---

## The Senate Abolition Amendment: https://docs.google.com/document/d/1BSmqCFprV0kKgQ5spWPNTCL4pXjztnRm08f5d0VxxnM/edit?usp=sharing by Calixte Edinburgh, introduced by Kosta Piastri
**Proposer:** kpokemon

### What Ilse Liked:
> The shift toward a unified parliamentary structure can increase accountability and streamline the legislative process, which is essential for effective social democratic governance and clear economic oversight.

### What Ilse Disliked:
> I am deeply concerned by the removal of the Senate, as an Ordoliberal I believe in strong institutional checks and balances; removing the upper house risks consolidating too much power and undermining the constitutional safeguards necessary to protect the market order and the rights of all citizens.

---

## To remove any ambiguity arising from the ruling in Li v. Caprica, introduce the following National-Regional Power Sharing (NRPS) Act:  All powers legislated and exercised by regional governments under Article VI, Section II(b) of the Constitution before the introduction of this bill are hereby delegated by the national government to the respective regions. Regional governments shall retain the authority to legislate within these areas concerning their own regions, unless such legislation conflicts with duly enacted national laws or future Acts of Parliament.
**Proposer:** alanmu0106

### What Ilse Liked:
> I support the clear delineation of powers that provides the legal certainty necessary for a functioning social market economy, preventing the regulatory chaos that hampers investment and social stability.

### What Ilse Disliked:
> I am concerned that the 'unless such legislation conflicts with future Acts of Parliament' clause leaves too much room for arbitrary national overreach, which could undermine the decentralization necessary for healthy, competitive federalism.

---

## CAARA
**Proposer:** draksicell

### What Ilse Liked:
> As an Ordoliberal, I appreciate the focus on measurable outcomes and institutional accountability through the mandatory reporting of reoffense rates, as well as the principle of providing vocational training which empowers the individual to re-enter the market as a productive member of society.

### What Ilse Disliked:
> I am concerned that the legislation lacks sufficient detail on the long-term fiscal sustainability of these state-funded programs and does not clearly define the parameters for 'public safety' thresholds, which risks creating inconsistent judicial application of sentence reductions.

---

## Crimes Amendment Act
**Proposer:** eruween

### What Ilse Liked:
> I strongly support the explicit expansion of the definition of 'exploitation' to include forced unlawful workplace conduct, as it provides a necessary Ordoliberal safeguard against the abuse of labor and reinforces the regulatory frameworks that protect the dignity of the working class.

### What Ilse Disliked:
> I am concerned that the expansion of citizen's arrest powers and the broad nature of 'disorderly' theft could lead to over-policing of social spaces; furthermore, the immunity provisions for undercover operations, despite judicial oversight, carry a significant risk of state-sanctioned overreach that threatens civil liberties.

---

## Comprehensive Migration Reform Act
**Proposer:** draksicell

### What Ilse Liked:
> I approve of the points-based assessment system for labor migrants, which correctly prioritizes economic productivity and labor-market demand, and the commitment to maintaining the rule of law through clear, time-bound judicial review processes.

### What Ilse Disliked:
> I am concerned that the 25% income tax rebate for labor migrants creates an unfair competitive distortion against domestic workers and potentially undermines the solidarity of our social tax base, which is contrary to sound Ordoliberal fiscal principles.

---

## AHAA!
**Proposer:** draksicell

### What Ilse Liked:
> I support the emphasis on individual autonomy and the provision of high-quality, universal healthcare, as a healthy and empowered citizenry is the bedrock of a functioning social market economy. The investment in R&D and the inclusion of private sector participation align with an ordoliberal approach to fostering innovation within a public framework.

### What Ilse Disliked:
> I am concerned that allowing medical procedures like HRT and puberty blockers to be performed at home without strict clinical supervision undermines medical standards and creates potential liability issues that could destabilize the Iechydda system. Furthermore, mandating that private insurers must cover all these services without clear actuarial assessments risks placing undue fiscal burdens on the private sector, which contradicts the principle of competitive neutrality.

---

## Amendment to GDNR: Add the following in section 2: No regulation, licensing condition or requirement, or administrative rule under this Act may be applied unless it is transparent, competitively neutral, and no more burdensome than necessary to protect service quality, public safety, infrastructure integrity, and consumer interests, nor may it unfairly favor a former monopoly provider, regional government-owned entity, or politically connected firm over other qualified competitors.
**Proposer:** alanmu0106

### What Ilse Liked:
> As an Ordoliberal, I strongly support the emphasis on competitive neutrality and the prevention of regulatory capture, which ensures that incumbent monopolies cannot use state power to stifle new, efficient market entrants.

### What Ilse Disliked:
> I am concerned that the 'no more burdensome than necessary' language could be exploited by corporate interests to challenge legitimate social and safety regulations, potentially undermining the state's capacity to uphold public service standards.

---

### What Ilse Disliked:

## Article 1, Section 2 of the Martial Law (Legislation) Act to read the following: “Following the imposition of martial law, certain legislation introduced to Parliament may be designated by the agreement of the leaders of both houses, the Prime Minister, **and the Leader of the Opposition** as pertinent to national security.
**Proposer:** uzkalnis

> The requirement for the Leader of the Opposition to consent provides a necessary check and balance against the concentration of executive power, aligning with the Ordoliberal principle of preventing arbitrary authority.

### What Ilse Disliked:
> The framework risks normalizing the suspension of normal parliamentary procedure, and it lacks sufficient transparency requirements regarding how 'pertinent to national security' is defined, which could be exploited to bypass public accountability.

## Vote of no confidence in Kosta Piastri with nomination of Jaco van Ryneveld as Prime Minister
**Proposer:** aalborg

### What Ilse Liked:
> I support the use of constitutional mechanisms to hold leadership accountable when the parliamentary process has reached a gridlock, as it upholds the rule of law within our democratic framework.

### What Ilse Disliked:
> The bill lacks a substantive policy agenda or economic reform plan; it is a purely procedural power shift that ignores the urgent need for a stabilized social market economy.

---

## Nominate Jaco van Ryneveld as Speaker of the House
**Proposer:** aalborg

### What Ilse Liked:
> Nothing

### What Ilse Disliked:
> The proposal provides no substantive information regarding the nominee's policy platform, professional transparency, or commitment to upholding the institutional integrity required by ordoliberal principles.

---

## House rules to allow a veto overturn to be proposed up to 3 times per parliamentary term. Apply this rule retroactively. By Rachel.
**Proposer:** asapientllama

### What Ilse Liked:
> The increase in legislative checks and balances could prevent executive overreach, which aligns with the Ordoliberal priority of maintaining a stable, rule-based legal framework that prevents the concentration of power.

### What Ilse Disliked:
> The retroactive application of these rules undermines legal certainty and predictability, two fundamental pillars of a functioning market economy and a stable state.

---

## Overturn the President’s veto on the Nuremberg Act
**Proposer:** alanmu0106

### What Ilse Liked:
> The legislative assertion of authority against executive overreach is a vital check-and-balance necessary for a stable, rule-based order.

### What Ilse Disliked:
> Without the specific text of the Nuremberg Act, I cannot endorse the substantive policy impacts, as Ordoliberalism requires that any legislation must ensure competitive markets and social security without arbitrary state intervention.

---

## CIVIC Act https://docs.google.com/document/d/1U7mGVVB63RhcCWrzPz3yOB6uyB0uhZZ204Ge5Zcp1SY/edit?usp=sharing
**Proposer:** alanmu0106

### What Ilse Liked:
> I strongly support the strict institutional separation of domestic law enforcement from national defense, as this limits the power of the state's security apparatus and reinforces the Ordoliberal principle of clear, rule-bound administrative spheres. Furthermore, enshrining due process and regional cooperation mandates helps protect the fundamental liberties of our working-class citizens from federal overreach.

### What Ilse Disliked:
> I am concerned that the concentration of investigative power under a single national director, even with Senate confirmation, risks creating a new bureaucratic monolith that could eventually mirror the very overreach we are trying to dismantle; the oversight mechanisms, while present, must be rigorous enough to prevent CIVIC from becoming a political tool.

---

## Overturn the President’s veto on the Nuremberg Act
**Proposer:** billy_clitoris

### What Ilse Liked:
> As an Ordoliberal, I support the concept of a clear, stable, and constitutionally sound legal framework that limits arbitrary executive power and ensures that the Rule of Law remains superior to the whims of the presidency.

### What Ilse Disliked:
> Without access to the specific text of the act, I am concerned that it may contain populist or overreaching provisions that could undermine the market competition and individual liberties that are essential for a healthy, democratic social order.

---

## Rename the position of Senate Majority Leader to Leader of the Government in the Senate (passed the Senate)
**Proposer:** qtvnickbro

### What Ilse Liked:
> The bill provides greater institutional transparency by clarifying the reporting lines and functional nature of the role, which adheres to the Ordoliberal preference for clear, defined legal frameworks.

### What Ilse Disliked:
> The change is largely symbolic and fails to address substantive issues of power consolidation or the urgent need for structural legislative reform to protect working-class interests.

---

## Technical and Further Education Act 2054 https://telegra.ph/Technical-and-Further-Education-Act-2054-06-10 (Passed in the Senate)
**Proposer:** qtvnickbro

### What Ilse Liked:
> I support the emphasis on human capital development and the creation of a structured pathway for the working class to acquire high-value technical skills, which strengthens our competitive social market economy.

### What Ilse Disliked:
> I am concerned that the bill may rely too heavily on top-down state control of curriculum, potentially stifling the flexibility needed for private industry to lead in defining necessary skill sets.

---

## Hold a binding referendum on polygamous marriages on the date of the next Caprican election. By Dick N Balls
**Proposer:** billy_clitoris

### What Ilse Liked:
> Nothing.

### What Ilse Disliked:
> As a proponent of Ordoliberal stability and focus on socio-economic structural reform, I find this bill to be a populist distraction that unnecessarily inflames cultural divisions rather than addressing the core democratic and economic issues facing our citizens.

---

## CEMA Act  https://docs.google.com/document/d/1Z268WBYc8EN0MJ_6Sm-j0zLzAFrsAZP6PJ55EUlccmw/edit?usp=sharing   Presented by Runmin Jiang
**Proposer:** alanmu0106

### What Ilse Liked:
> I strongly support the emphasis on civilian control, parliamentary oversight, and the requirement for independent audits of emergency funds, which ensures that state power remains checked and transparent in line with Ordoliberal principles.

### What Ilse Disliked:
> While the framework is sound, I am concerned about the scope of 'expedited' emergency procurement; we must ensure that the urgency of a crisis is not used as a loophole to bypass fair competition laws and invite cronyism.

---

## The Nuremberg Act. Presented by Runmin Jiang
**Proposer:** alanmu0106

### What Ilse Liked:
> Nothing.

### What Ilse Disliked:
> I cannot provide a formal analysis of a bill that lacks substantive content; transparency and clarity are fundamental requirements for the democratic process and a stable ordoliberal market order.

---

## Launch a parliamentary inquiry into COIN overreach and abuses against the constitution. By Rachel Edelstein-Powell.
**Proposer:** asapientllama

### What Ilse Liked:
> I support the fundamental Ordoliberal necessity of subjecting state security organs to the rule of law, ensuring that constitutional boundaries are maintained to protect the social order from arbitrary power.

### What Ilse Disliked:
> The lack of specific procedural safeguards in the bill creates a risk that the inquiry could devolve into mere political theater rather than a rigorous, evidence-based assessment of institutional failure.

---

## Amend Article VI Section II of the constitution with following changes of clauses:  Amend §II(a) as such: After "exclusive jurisdiction over the matters of" change the following text of the clause to read "regional education system administration, regions' national guard, local welfare systems, infrastructure of local importance, regions' internal trade and insurance, and elections of the local authorities and local administrative apparatus."  Amend §II(b) as such: Between "taxation," and "environmental protection" add "healthcare, education,". After "established by the Parliament of Caprica." add "Regions' jurisdiction in these spheres is subject to the precedence of federal law."  Amend §II(c) as such: Before "and currency regulation" add "healthcare, education, national infrastructure, national census, foreign trade, maritime transport vessel registry, banking, patents, copyright, criminal law, and such matters of national competency not outlined in Article VI §II(a)"
**Proposer:** alanmu0106

### What Ilse Liked:
> I approve of the clear constitutional codification of federal precedence in areas like healthcare, education, and banking, which ensures a unified economic and social framework essential for maintaining a competitive, rule-based Ordoliberal market order.

### What Ilse Disliked:
> I am concerned that by centralizing broad powers such as 'criminal law' and 'national infrastructure' without explicitly defined federal standards, we risk over-bureaucratization that could stifle local accountability and undermine the necessary regional autonomy required for effective local welfare administration.

---

## Amend above to remove "Exit Project OPLANA."
**Proposer:** aalborg

### What Ilse Liked:
> I support the preservation of institutional continuity and the protection of long-term strategic projects that provide stability to the Caprican social market, provided they are managed with transparency and fiscal responsibility.

### What Ilse Disliked:
> Without the full text of the project's charter, I am concerned that removing this exit clause may signal a lack of sunset provisions, which are essential to ensuring that state-supported initiatives remain accountable to the public and subject to market-based performance benchmarks.

---

## Exit Project OPLANA. Cancel the last 2 Montiablo SSNs. Rename Caprican People’s Self Defence Liberation Force to Caprican Self Defense Force. Scrap section 4 of Caprican People’s Self Defence Liberation Force Expansion Act for its conflict with the new constitutional amendment.
**Proposer:** alanmu0106

### What Ilse Liked:
> I support the effort to bring the Caprican Self Defense Force into clear constitutional alignment and the commitment to administrative transparency regarding internal security projects.

### What Ilse Disliked:
> I am concerned that the summary lacks specific details on the socio-economic impacts of cancelling these SSNs and the potential erosion of social safeguards that provide stability for our working-class citizens.

---

## Constitution amendment. Add the following to Article III, Section II:The State shall not initiate the use of nuclear weapons under any circumstance, except in response to a nuclear weapon attack upon the domestic territory of the State. For the purposes of this Article, “domestic territory” shall mean the seventeen regions of the State as recognized under this Constitution.
**Proposer:** alanmu0106

### What Ilse Liked:
> The bill provides a clear, constitutional check on state power, which aligns with the Ordoliberal principle of ensuring the state operates within a predictable and rule-bound framework to prevent the arbitrary use of force.

### What Ilse Disliked:
> The strict limitation could potentially undermine the state's capacity to protect the collective security necessary for a stable social market economy, as it restricts defensive options during unconventional or existential threats.

---

## Local Government Fiscal Responsibility Act. Introduced by Runmin Jiang
**Proposer:** alanmu0106

### What Ilse Liked:
> As an Ordoliberal, I appreciate the emphasis on fiscal discipline and creating a stable, predictable legal framework for local economic management, which prevents the build-up of unsustainable debt that ultimately harms the citizenry.

### What Ilse Disliked:
> I am concerned that without specific safeguards, these mandates could lead to austerity measures that disproportionately reduce essential public services for the working class, potentially undermining the social safety net we strive to maintain.

---

> If the implementation lacks a framework for ensuring that students also achieve proficiency in the national language, it could inadvertently create barriers to social mobility and hinder integration into the broader competitive labor market, which contradicts Ordoliberal principles of equal opportunity.

### What Ilse Disliked:
> I am concerned that the regional decentralization of education standards without robust central oversight risks creating fragmented skill levels across Caprica, potentially hindering the free movement of labor and equal opportunity that a strong Ordoliberal market requires.

## Amend section 2 subsection 4 of coin Renewal Act to: Article 4 — The Ministry of Defense shall retain authority to initiate, develop, procure, and manage weapons programs, military equipment, research projects, and defense modernization efforts as it deems necessary for national security.  Parliament shall not be required to authorize the creation or commencement of such programs unless legislation specifically mandates fixed quantities of ships, aircraft, vehicles, weapons, personnel units, or other military assets.  Nothing in this Article shall prevent Parliament from appropriating funds, conducting oversight, or passing legislation setting binding force structure requirements.
**Proposer:** alanmu0106

### What Ilse Liked:
> The bill maintains the ultimate parliamentary power of the purse and the authority to set binding force structure requirements, which provides a necessary check on executive overreach.

### What Ilse Disliked:
> The reduction of parliamentary oversight in the initiation phase risks creating a 'state within a state' where defense spending becomes opaque, undermining the transparency and market-based competition essential to a healthy Ordoliberal order.

## Firearm Permit System Act, 2053
**Proposer:** aalborg

### What Ilse Disliked:
> If the bill imposes excessive bureaucratic costs or arbitrary barriers that disproportionately burden the working class's ability to defend their property without providing a commensurate increase in public safety, it fails to achieve the necessary balance between individual liberty and social stability.

## Property Expropriation Act, 2053

> As an Ordoliberal, I fundamentally oppose the arbitrary seizure of private assets, as it destabilizes the secure property rights that are the bedrock of a functioning market economy. ## Amend section 2 subsection 7, remove the subclause 'so long as it doesn't promote things such as extremism or hate'

## TV and Movie Classification Act (https://docs.google.com/document/d/1aBzExV88z2LkRI1QgJOA0l-QaQyMmc3_bpRabgv5v6s/edit?tab=t.0)
**Proposer:** portadelaidejunkie

### What Ilse Liked:
> I appreciate the transparency of creating a clear, codified framework that informs citizens and parents of content expectations, which is essential for a functioning, informed social market.

### What Ilse Disliked:
> The bill grants the government excessive discretionary power over artistic expression and imposes an overly burdensome, interventionist regulatory process on private creative industries that threatens market efficiency and free expression.

---

## Illegalise the advertising of alcohol and mandate that there be at least 1 health warning per packaging.
**Proposer:** portadelaidejunkie

### What Ilse Liked:
> I support the mandate for clear health warnings, as it empowers citizens with transparent information to make responsible, informed choices in a functioning market.

### What Ilse Disliked:
> The blanket ban on advertising restricts the legitimate freedom of contract and competition among producers, which risks stifling market innovation without necessarily addressing the underlying root causes of substance abuse.

---

## Illegalise the advertising of cigarettes and mandate that there be at least 2 health risk warnings per package (Auscoded)
**Proposer:** portadelaidejunkie

### What Ilse Liked:
> I support the use of clear, transparent information to empower consumer choice and the proactive approach to reducing long-term public health costs, which aligns with the state's role in creating a healthy, functioning market environment.

### What Ilse Disliked:
> I am concerned that the prohibition of advertising, while well-intentioned, represents an unnecessary state intervention into private enterprise; the focus should remain on informing the consumer rather than restricting the legal right of a business to conduct trade.

---

## Illegalise the advertising of gambling.
**Proposer:** portadelaidejunkie

### What Ilse Liked:
> I support this as a necessary market correction to protect the social fabric, as predatory gambling industries exploit the working class and undermine the personal responsibility required for a healthy, functioning social market economy.

### What Ilse Disliked:
> I worry that a blanket ban may inadvertently create a black market, and I would prefer to see this paired with robust regulatory frameworks that ensure competition remains fair and transparent rather than just resorting to absolute state prohibition.

---

## drugs act (https://docs.google.com/document/d/1eNzZegcuJ1ar4rVEJO9oKCZa5w8CA2RKm-423PBxsGY/edit?tab=t.0)
**Proposer:** portadelaidejunkie

### What Ilse Liked:
> I approve of the move to bring the cannabis market out of the shadows and into a regulated framework with excise taxes, as this aligns with an Ordoliberal preference for transparent, state-monitored competition. Furthermore, the focus on mandatory rehabilitation for addicts rather than immediate incarceration for personal possession reflects a compassionate Social Democratic approach to public health.

### What Ilse Disliked:
> I am concerned that the mandate for rehabilitation to last until 'the rehab runner sees fit' grants excessive, unchecked discretionary power to state agents, which undermines the rule of law and lacks the necessary legal predictability. Additionally, the broad 30-year sentencing guidelines for distribution are excessively punitive and fail to reflect a nuanced market-based approach to different levels of narcotics activity.

---

## "rules to require that a notification is sent to all MPs after every bill is posted to encourage them to actually show up to work. (meta: we start pinging them)" - By CJ Edinburgh, introduced by Kosta Piastri
**Proposer:** kpokemon

### What Ilse Liked:
> I support the emphasis on administrative transparency and the expectation that elected representatives fulfill their fundamental duty to attend proceedings and engage with the legislative process.

### What Ilse Disliked:
> The bill borders on performative bureaucracy; true parliamentary engagement requires institutional reform and political will rather than mere digital pings, which may simply clutter communication channels without addressing the root causes of absenteeism.

## Amend Section 4(11) of the Life Autonomy Act (https://discord.com/channels/1189603606568108103/1189604125911044279/1487840374230745222) by adding section 4(12):  "For patients over the age of 18:  a) A request shall be subject to mandatory assessment by an independent psychiatrist, who must confirm decision-making capacity and the absence of treatable mental disorders influencing the request;  (b) a mandatory reflection period of no less than 30 days shall apply, unless the patient’s condition is deemed urgent by all reviewing physicians."
**Proposer:** aalborg

### What Ilse Liked:
> The emphasis on independent, evidence-based psychiatric assessment aligns with the Ordoliberal requirement for a structured, rule-based framework that prevents arbitrary decisions while protecting the individual's autonomy through thorough verification of capacity.

### What Ilse Disliked:
> The potential for this framework to evolve into an overly bureaucratic barrier that undermines the dignity of the individual and limits self-determination in cases of chronic, non-urgent suffering.

## Remove euthanasia for persons under the age of 18 except for the case of severe untreatable illness
**Proposer:** portadelaidejunkie

### What Ilse Liked:
> The focus on protecting vulnerable minors through strict legal safeguards aligns with the Ordoliberal requirement for a robust state-defined framework to protect human dignity and autonomy.

### What Ilse Disliked:
> The bill lacks specific definitions for 'severe untreatable illness,' which could lead to administrative ambiguity and potentially undermine the rule of law regarding medical practice.

## Capital Park-Redmont Rail Corridor Act, 2052
**Proposer:** uzkalnis

### What Ilse Liked:
> I support the emphasis on state-led infrastructure investment that fosters market efficiency by connecting labor pools to employment centers, as well as the adherence to open, competitive procurement procedures which prevent cronyism.

### What Ilse Disliked:
> The bill lacks a clear framework for long-term private-sector competition on the tracks, which could lead to a Caprilink monopoly, and I am concerned that the 80% federal funding cap might place an undue fiscal burden on regional governments if cost overruns occur.

## Amend Section 1 (4) of the Harmful Substances Act to read: Selling weed is to be illegal and will result in a prison sentence of up to 5-years
**Proposer:** nikderprager

### What Ilse Liked:
> Nothing.

### What Ilse Disliked:
> As an Ordoliberal, I object to this bill because it utilizes punitive criminal measures rather than market regulation, and as a Social Democrat, I find the mandatory sentencing guidelines overly draconian, failing to address the socio-economic root causes of substance use.

## Amend Section 1(6) of the Harmful Substances Act to read: There is to be no charge for personal use or ownership of illicit substances under a limit of 30 grams for soft drugs and 3 grams for hard drugs.
**Proposer:** uzkalnis

### What Ilse Liked:
> The focus on harm reduction and the potential to reduce the burden on our judicial and correctional systems, which allows for a more social-democratic approach to public health rather than mere criminalization.

### What Ilse Disliked:
> The lack of robust regulatory frameworks to accompany the policy; as an ordoliberal, I believe that if the state facilitates market access to substances, it must enforce stringent competition and safety rules to prevent market failure and protect the social order.

## Federal Ticketing Act; Section 4 (2) to the following: RTAs should only have exclusive authority over prices and fares only within their respective regions, provided that nothing in this Act shall prohibit the establishment of a nationwide public transportation ticketing program and remove Section 3 (3)
**Proposer:** nikderprager

### What Ilse Liked:
> I support the emphasis on regional autonomy, as it aligns with Ordoliberal principles of competition and local accountability, provided the national framework ensures equal access and efficiency for the working class.

### What Ilse Disliked:
> The removal of Section 3 (3) is concerning, as it lacks transparency regarding how the transition to regional pricing will be regulated to prevent price gouging or the erosion of affordable transit options for lower-income citizens.

## AHAA!, 2052. By Angela Sky-Rose and Ingrid Wolf-van Ryneveld
**Proposer:** matiyuaaa

### What Ilse Liked:
> Nothing

### What Ilse Disliked:
> I cannot support or critique a bill that lacks documentation; as a policymaker, I require full transparency to ensure legislation aligns with the principles of a social market economy.

## Harmful Substances Act (https://docs.google.com/document/d/1ZHqtDbHOzNaxQM1x5T85xu4iS_vVCuh9MAzSodfscfg/edit?tab=t.0)
**Proposer:** portadelaidejunkie

### What Ilse Liked:
> I appreciate the movement toward decriminalization for personal possession, as it shifts the focus from punitive carceral measures toward mandatory rehabilitation, reflecting a more humane social policy that supports the well-being of the working class.

### What Ilse Disliked:
> The bill creates a dangerous market contradiction by decriminalizing personal use of cannabis while imposing draconian prison sentences for its sale; an Ordoliberal approach requires a regulated, legal market framework to prevent black-market violence, rather than relying on heavy-handed prohibition that ignores the economic realities of supply and demand.

---

## Federal Ticketing Act, 2052
**Proposer:** uzkalnis

### What Ilse Liked:
> I support the emphasis on technical standardization and interoperability, which reduces friction for the working class traveling across regions; furthermore, the use of federal grants for modernization respects the Ordoliberal principle of providing infrastructure foundations without infringing on the decentralized market autonomy of Regional Transport Authorities.

### What Ilse Disliked:
> I am wary of the creation of the Federal Transport Clearing Agency (FTCA); while its scope is currently limited, the centralization of financial settlement processes risks bureaucratic overreach that could eventually compromise the fiscal independence of regional transport systems.

---

## age of sexual consent laws to raise the age of consent to 18. While keeping in place the previously established Romeo and remove section 1 subsection 1 C (https://docs.google.com/document/d/1sPSp0CaFZfv2mKhqGkW__onF6t3e_x_mtQI8iX_Ql5Y/edit?tab=t.0)
**Proposer:** portadelaidejunkie

### What Ilse Liked:
> I approve of the state-funded mental health support for grooming victims and the imposition of corporate accountability for platforms that fail to report grooming, as these protect the dignity and safety of our citizens.

### What Ilse Disliked:
> The ambiguity in Section 1, subsection 3 creates a lack of legal certainty—a core tenet of Ordoliberalism—by leaving significant criminal liability to the subjective discretion of a judge and jury rather than clear, consistent statutes.

## Cyber Incident Reporting for Critical Infrastructure Act (https://docs.google.com/document/d/1vTWlpnlNtnPof9WseoXNcLkhd8dboLxZDMVNVLPbhd8/edit?usp=sharing) by Sen. Isolde von Dittarsdorf - passed the Senate
**Proposer:** astero9890_

### What Ilse Liked:
> I support the emphasis on institutional transparency and the state's role in protecting the market order from systemic threats; clear reporting mandates provide the necessary data for a stable, functioning economic framework without undue regulatory overreach.

### What Ilse Disliked:
> I am concerned that the 'good faith' liability protections are somewhat vague and could inadvertently shield corporations from proper accountability if they fail to maintain robust security protocols in the first place.

---

## Edelstein-Wolf Electoral Act to make all 25 seats in the House of Caprica elected via closed party list proportional representation with 4% national threshold and seats distributed using the dhondt method  - passed the Senate
**Proposer:** kyungsoo334

### What Ilse Liked:
> The move toward a proportional representation system is a welcome step, as it ensures that the legislature more accurately reflects the pluralistic voice of the working class and prevents the distortions caused by majoritarianism.

### What Ilse Disliked:
> The use of closed party lists threatens to consolidate too much power within party hierarchies, reducing the influence of individual candidates and potentially stifling the democratic accountability that is essential to a healthy, ordoliberal market society.

---

## Caprican Agricultural Cooperatives Reestablishment Act of 2050. By Ingrid Wolf-van Ryneveld, 2050.
**Proposer:** matiyuaaa

### What Ilse Liked:
> I support the emphasis on self-governance and competitive cooperation among small producers, which aligns with the Ordoliberal principle of protecting a decentralized market order from monopolistic pressures.

### What Ilse Disliked:
> I am concerned that without strict regulatory guardrails, these cooperatives could evolve into cartels that distort price signals or unfairly exclude independent farmers from the broader Caprican market.

---

### What Ilse Disliked:
> I am concerned that this creates unnecessary bureaucratic layering, which threatens the efficient use of public funds and risks fracturing the essential link between a robust public education system and the scientific advancements that should serve the working class.

> The bill displays a complete lack of legislative decorum and fiscal discipline; the unprofessional language in the text is unacceptable, and the lack of a clear, coherent framework for competitive market oversight or long-term budget accountability suggests a chaotic, ad-hoc approach to economic planning rather than the ordoliberal principles of a well-ordered, rule-based social market economy.

> The motion lacks a detailed policy alternative beyond a change in personnel, and replacing leadership without a pre-established commitment to protecting the social safety net risks instability that harms the working class.

## Edelstein-Wolf Electoral Act Section ii(i) to “Parliament may only be dissolved outside its regularly scheduled elections by a two-thirds vote in favour in both houses, or if no Prime Minister is appointed within a year of the previous elections. ~~This will not disrupt the regularly scheduled elections and will only elect for the term until the next planned elections.~~ In the event of a snap election, following elections shall be rescheduled accordingly.”. This will also mean the next house election shall take place around 2052, rather than 2050.

### What Ilse Disliked:

**Proposer:** aalborg

## Edelstein-Wolf Electoral Reform Act and restore proportional representation in the House with 17 seats
**Proposer:** uzkalnis

### What Ilse Liked:
> I strongly support the restoration of proportional representation, as it ensures that the diverse voices of the working class and minority political interests are accurately reflected in our legislative process, preventing the consolidation of power that often accompanies majoritarian systems.

> I am concerned that a rigid, small number of only 17 seats may prove insufficient for proper representational granularity in a modern democracy, potentially diluting the impact of smaller parties and hindering the effective competition necessary for a functioning social market economy.

### What Ilse Disliked:
> I am deeply concerned that a simple majority requirement invites political instability and executive overreach, as it may incentivize opportunistic dissolution of the legislature rather than stable, rule-based governance essential to the Ordoliberal order.

### What Ilse Disliked:
> I am deeply concerned that the rigid 30% lower bound on debt-to-GDP effectively forbids the government from paying down debt even during times of prosperity, and the arbitrary percentage caps on ministerial expenditure volatility risk hampering the state's ability to respond dynamically to unforeseen social needs.

## Transport Market Devolution Act, 2040

### What Ilse Disliked:

## West Montiablo Bypass Act.

## Raudea Agreement 2038 
**Proposer:** nikderprager

### What Ilse Liked:
> I welcome the commitment to the rule of law and the promotion of democratic values, as well as the ordoliberal focus on establishing a stable, rules-based framework for international trade that repairs the damage caused by the previous administration.

### What Ilse Disliked:
> The reduction of tariffs on luxury goods feels like a concession to elites that does little for the working class, and I am concerned that the agreement lacks specific, enforceable social protections to prevent a 'race to the bottom' in labor standards within this new free trade area.

## Nomination of Jean-François Le Moyne to fill the Vice Presidency
**Proposer:** aalborg

### What Ilse Liked:

---

## Competitive Energy Market Act (CEMA), 2037
**Proposer:** uzkalnis

### What Ilse Liked:
> As an Ordoliberal, I support the unbundling of the state energy monopoly to restore competitive market mechanisms, which prevent the inefficiencies of state-run cartels while ensuring the Energy Regulatory Authority retains the power to supervise the market framework and energy security.

### What Ilse Disliked:
> I am deeply concerned by the repeal of statutory board representation requirements, which undermines the Social Democrat commitment to worker participation in governance, and I am wary of the potential for regulatory capture if the unbundling is not paired with robust protections for low-income consumers against market volatility.

---

## Competitive Markets Act (CMA), 2037
**Proposer:** uzkalnis

### What Ilse Liked:
> I support the emphasis on creating an efficient, competitive market framework, as the breaking up of monolithic state-owned enterprises into independent, market-based entities is a fundamental Ordoliberal principle to prevent cartelization and ensure the rule of law within the economy.

### What Ilse Disliked:
> I strongly condemn the repeal of worker-governance structures and the AI transition protections without establishing a robust, permanent social safety net; an Ordoliberal order requires that the state provide a 'social floor' to protect citizens from market volatility, and stripping these protections without clear, codified replacements threatens the very social stability essential for a healthy market economy.

---

## National Legislature Overhaul Amendment
**Proposer:** uzkalnis

### What Ilse Liked:
> I appreciate the Ordoliberal focus on institutional stability and constitutional safeguards provided by a second chamber, which acts as a check against the populist impulses that can threaten the rule of law.

### What Ilse Disliked:
> I am deeply concerned by the removal of age limits for lawmakers, as the inclusion of elders in government is essential for legislative wisdom, and I fear the Senate's power to reject legislation may create gridlock that hinders the Social Democratic mandate to act swiftly for the working class.

---

## Raudea Agreement 2038 
**Proposer:** nikderprager

### What Ilse Liked:
> I support the commitment to a rules-based economic order and the reduction of trade barriers, which aligns with the Ordoliberal principle of market competition, as well as the investment in education and sustainable infrastructure which supports the long-term well-being of the working class.

### What Ilse Disliked:
> I am concerned by the reduction of tariffs on luxury goods, which risks exacerbating social inequality, and I worry that the lack of specific protections for workers' rights and collective bargaining mechanisms within the trade framework may undermine the social market economy.

## **This House formally censures Rt. Hon. Max Palme MP** for conduct inconsistent with the orderly functioning of Parliament, including attempts to obstruct committee operations and undermine confidence in the legislative process. This censure affirms Parliament’s authority to conduct its business in accordance with the Constitution and standing rules, and serves as a formal expression of disapproval of conduct that disrupts its proper functioning.
**Proposer:** uzkalnis

### What Ilse Liked:
> As an Ordoliberal, I support this censure because the rule of law and the sanctity of established procedural frameworks are essential to a functioning market democracy; conduct that disrupts the constitutional order threatens the very institutions that protect our citizens' rights.

### What Ilse Disliked:
> Nothing.

---

## Electoral System Act, 2037
**Proposer:** uzkalnis

### What Ilse Liked:
> The use of closed-list proportional representation for Parliament promotes strong party platforms and collective accountability, which aligns with my commitment to organized, stable democratic institutions that serve the working class.

### What Ilse Disliked:
> The Hare quota with no stated electoral threshold risks extreme fragmentation of the legislature, which creates weak coalitions and hinders the orderly, consistent governance required by a healthy market-social order.

---

## National Legislature Overhaul Amendment, 2037
**Proposer:** uzkalnis

### What Ilse Liked:
> I appreciate the inclusion of an upper chamber tasked with safeguarding constitutional order and long-term institutional stability, which acts as a necessary ordoliberal check on the potential volatility of the lower chamber. The non-partisan nature of the Senate aligns with the goal of protecting the common good from narrow partisan interests.

### What Ilse Disliked:
> I am concerned that the small number of Senators and their veto power over legislation could lead to legislative gridlock, effectively stalling essential social democratic reforms. Furthermore, placing the Vice President as the presiding officer of the Senate risks politicizing what should be a strictly impartial deliberative body.

---

## Notwithstanding the Parliamentary Committees Act (1989), the provisions of Section 4 requiring prior committee review are hereby suspended for the consideration of the following four bills: (1)  National Legislature Overhaul Amendment, 2037; (2)  Electoral System Act, 2037; (3)  Competitive Markets Act (CMA), 2037; (4)  Competitive Energy Market Act (CEMA), 2037
**Proposer:** uzkalnis

### What Ilse Liked:
> I support the intention behind the Competitive Markets Act and the Competitive Energy Market Act, as breaking up monopolies and fostering genuine market competition is essential to an Ordoliberal framework that prevents the concentration of private power.

### What Ilse Disliked:
> The suspension of the Parliamentary Committees Act is a dangerous subversion of democratic due process; the Social Democratic commitment to transparency and the rule of law requires rigorous committee oversight to ensure these reforms truly serve the public interest rather than consolidating executive power.

---

## Electoral System Act, 2037
**Proposer:** uzkalnis

### What Ilse Liked:
> I approve of the proportional representation system for Parliament, as it ensures that the legislative body better reflects the diverse voices and interests of the working class compared to majoritarian systems.

### What Ilse Disliked:
> I am deeply concerned by the use of closed-party lists for Parliament, which centralizes power within party hierarchies and strips the voter of their right to hold individual candidates directly accountable to their constituents.

---

## National Legislature Overhaul Amendment, 2037
**Proposer:** uzkalnis

### What Ilse Liked:
> I appreciate the inclusion of institutional checks and balances, specifically the Senate's role in judicial appointments and constitutional oversight, which aligns with the Ordoliberal priority of maintaining a stable, rule-based legal framework that prevents the concentration of power.

### What Ilse Disliked:
> I am deeply concerned by the reduction of the Parliament to only seventeen members, which risks weakening the representation of the working class and diluting the democratic connection between the citizens and their government, potentially leading to elite capture of the legislative process.

### What Ilse Disliked:
> I am concerned that the bill relies too heavily on rigid, punitive carceral solutions rather than addressing the socio-economic root causes of crime, and the broad sentencing ranges risk undermining judicial discretion and the principle of proportionality.

---

## Columbian Free Movement Act, 2030 https://docs.google.com/document/d/16xiGXjTZ_2tBB0gcZx7I1DcJOXsuGx8B9uV1dWg-nPM/edit?usp=sharing

### What Ilse Disliked:
> I am concerned that this bill lacks explicit mechanisms to protect the Caprican labor market from downward wage pressure, and I fear the reliance on a supra-national entity for visa and border criteria may compromise our national sovereignty and the social stability necessary for our working-class citizens.

### What Ilse Disliked:
> I am concerned that the sentencing structure relies heavily on 'up-to' phrasing, which creates excessive judicial discretion that could lead to inconsistent application of the law, undermining the predictability essential to Ordoliberal principles.

## Begin construction of HSR phases 1B, 2A, and 2B and the Central Ambrosia and Central Mezata highways from the Keep Caprica Moving Act

## End of Life Choice Act, 2028
**Proposer:** uzkalnis

### What Ilse Liked:
> I appreciate the robust institutional framework and legal safeguards provided, such as mandatory independent medical oversight and the explicit protection of a citizen's right to refuse participation based on conscience, which aligns with the Ordoliberal principle of a well-defined legal order protecting individual agency.

### What Ilse Disliked:
> I am concerned that the scope of 'unbearable suffering'—specifically the inclusion of psychological suffering—is dangerously broad and lacks the precise, objective criteria required to prevent systemic abuse or the erosion of the state's duty to provide adequate social and mental health support for the most vulnerable.

## Amendment the Energy Market Liberalization Act: Section 4: (5) The Government shall maintain oversight of electricity generation assets supplying or located within designated national security zones. (6) The Energy Regulatory Authority (ERA), in consultation with the Ministry of Defence, the Caprica Office of Intelligence (COIN), and the Ministry of Finance, shall: (a) Vet the ownership and control of electricity generation assets supplying critical military or strategic infrastructure. (b) Prohibit or condition any transfer of ownership deemed to pose a national security risk. (7) The Government may maintain or operate backup generation units within military facilities for emergency use.
**Proposer:** uzkalnis

### What Ilse Liked:
> As an Ordoliberal, I appreciate the clear establishment of a regulatory framework that addresses 'market failure' in national security, ensuring that private ownership does not compromise the state's functional integrity. It provides a transparent, rule-based mechanism for vetting strategic infrastructure rather than arbitrary state interference.

### What Ilse Disliked:
> I am concerned that the language regarding 'conditioning' ownership transfers could be used as a pretext for excessive bureaucratic overreach or protectionism, potentially stifling the competitive efficiency of the energy market if not strictly tethered to concrete security criteria.

---

## National Finals Exam Act to include the following. Section 2.9; the exams may also be taken in the Myrati language. Section 2.10; No subjects are to be compulsory. > I am concerned about the potential for fiscal mismanagement and the lack of a clear competitive bidding process, which must be strictly regulated to prevent state-sponsored monopolies from inflating costs at the taxpayer's expense.

---

## Hold a referendum during this parliament, on a date of the CEC's choosing, that must be announced, at most, 24 hours after the passage of this motion, asking: 1. Should the Caprican presidential term be shortened to 4 years? 2. If the term is shortened, should the next presidential election be held in 2004, at the same time as the next general election, or in 2006, halfway through the parliamentary term?
**Proposer:** billy_clitoris

### What Ilse Liked:
> The emphasis on direct democracy and citizen participation in constitutional matters aligns with the need for strong, accountable institutions that reflect the public will.

### What Ilse Disliked:
> The rushed timeline for announcing the referendum date—within 24 hours—lacks the necessary deliberative process and transparent policy-making required for stable governance, potentially undermining the orderly operation of our state.

---

## Hold a referendum during this parliament, on a date of the CEC's choosing, that must be announced, at most, 24 hours after the passage of this motion, asking: 1. Should the Caprican presidential term be shortened to 4 years, in line with the parliamentary term?
**Proposer:** billy_clitoris

### What Ilse Liked:
> I support the emphasis on democratic legitimacy and institutional alignment, as periodic synchronization ensures greater accountability of the executive to the legislative process.

### What Ilse Disliked:
> The extremely short, 24-hour window for announcing the date is procedurally reckless and undermines the stability and transparency required for a functioning social market order.

---

## Unemployment Insurance Act
**Proposer:** grey_72355

> I strongly support the principle of a mandatory insurance scheme that provides a reliable 'floor' for workers, as it fosters both individual dignity and the social stability required for a healthy market economy.

### What Ilse Disliked:
> If the bill lacks strict actuarial soundness or creates a permanent dependency trap that discourages workforce participation, I would oppose it, as it must maintain competition and incentivize re-employment rather than merely funding idleness.

## State Energy Corporation Act's Section 5(1)(c) to read the following: All existing fossil fuel and gas production plants will be decommissioned by December 31, ~~2021~~ 2040. Amend Section 6(1) to read the following: The SEC will prioritise the construction and expansion of Renewable and Storage Technologies whereby at least 50% of Energy Generation in Caprica is renewable energy by the year ~~2010~~ 2018, and 75% by the year ~~2018~~ 2030.
**Proposer:** uzkalnis

### What Ilse Liked:
> I support the clear commitment to environmental sustainability and the state's role in facilitating a strategic infrastructure shift that ensures long-term energy security for Caprican workers.

### What Ilse Disliked:
> The timeline for the 75% renewable target is overly ambitious and risks market volatility; failing to provide a robust transition plan for the labor force currently employed in fossil fuel extraction violates my commitment to social stability and Ordoliberal principles of managed economic transformation.

---

## State Energy Corporation Act's Section 5(1)(c) to read the following: All existing fossil fuel and gas production plants will be decommissioned by December 31, ~~2021~~ **2030**. Amend Section 6(1) to read the following: The SEC will prioritise the construction and expansion of Renewable and Storage Technologies whereby at least 50% of Energy Generation in Caprica is renewable energy by the year ~~2010~~ 2018, and 75% by the year ~~2018~~ 2030.

Arbitrary nationalization threatens the economic security of the working class by destabilizing private ownership, which serves as the ultimate safeguard against state overreach.
